The 8 Best Refrigerators to Keep Your Food Fresh and Organized

📅 2021-03-25  |  Read Source →

We gathered the latest picks from popular brands like Samsung, Maytag, and more.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

A refrigerator is intended to do only three things: preserve food, store it properly, and supply enough lighting to see all contents. We also want our fridge to be reliable and last a long time so we don’t have to go through this exhaustive shopping exercise again until it’s absolutely necessary.

When shopping for a refrigerator, we're not just looking for something that keeps our food cold. We require aesthetic appeal from our primary kitchen appliance. We also crave convenience in the form of spacious and adjustable shelves and bins, temperature or humidity-controlled spaces, an ice maker so we can ditch the refillable trays, and whatever other clever additions fridge makers come up with to lure us into spending more cash.

If you haven’t shopped for a refrigerator in a while, we’ve got some good news and some bad news. The bad news is that refrigerators may be more expensive due to ongoing tariffs on both foreign steel and aluminum used to manufacture models in the U.S. and on wholly imported fridges. The good news, though, is that there is a wider variety of designs and sizes to match your family’s specific needs, plus features like exterior or interior water and ice dispensing options, separate food-specific cooling spaces, and an abundance of smart tech.

To help you find the perfect fridge for your kitchen, we researched a variety of options based on specific criteria and found the best of the best. We also made sure our picks all varied in terms of style, so below, you'll find our top recommendations for French door refrigerators, side-by-side fridges, and a number of other types, as well.

As with any other appliance, our consideration for best models was primarily based on value—capacity and features for the money. Value also incorporates the reputation of the manufacturer and opinions from both retailers and consumers (from online reviews). We also compared capacities and feature sets of various models and took into account our own hands-on experiences at appliance stores and trade shows over the years.

When faced with seemingly equal choices, especially on price, we always leaned toward higher-capacity models since we know there’s no such thing as too much refrigerator space. However, we also recognize that higher-capacity refrigerators are usually full or standard depth models that may be too big for many kitchens, so we’ve tried to add less pricey, smaller alternatives where we found them.

Stewart Wolpin wrote the initial version of this article—he's covered consumer technology, including home entertainment and appliances, since the early 1980s. Amber Joglar, a technology and home appliance editor with PM, handled the latest update to this roundup.

While there is a plethora of premium refrigerators priced $5,000 and up—and some priced $15,000 and more—we decided to keep our “Best” choices under $5,000.

If ensuring your food stays cool is at the top of your list when shopping for a refrigerator, then your best bet is investing in this four-door Samsung model. It's equipped with AI hybrid cooling, which essentially means using just electricity, sans refrigerant, via a semiconductor-based component.

This fridge automatically cools down to compensate for when you leave the door open too long. As a result, Samsung claims that meat and seafood are preserved a smidge longer, and your fridge’s overall power usage can be lowered by up to 10 percent.

Beyond this cooling technology, the Samsung offers plenty of convenient features. The beverage center inside the left top door provides a tall water dispenser, a 48-ounce autofill water pitcher with infuser, and a removable nozzle for cleaning. The dual ice maker behind the bottom left door makes both ice cubes and ice spheres.

The fridge’s doors open via gentle touch rather than forcing you to tug on handles, which is useful for when your arms are filled with groceries. You can also set the multi-pantry drawer for the correct cooling conditions to properly preserve fruit and veggies, beverages, or meat and fish.

This Samsung counter-depth four-door has a generous 22.5 cubic feet of capacity and 13.8 percent more cooling capability than previous comparable Samsung models.

This GE fridge is about as basic and uncomplicated as it gets, which explains why it only costs around $800 after discount. What makes this fridge a bargain is its substantial 21.9 cubic feet of storage space, which is more than a majority of other entry-level top-freezer fridges we considered.

One of its door shelves accommodates gallon jugs, and the included bins are modular to adjust to different jar, bottle, and container heights. Temperature controls are straightforward and easy to access on the roof of the fridge. It also includes a Sabbath mode to eliminate all the fridge’s electrical responses when the door is opened. Plus, it’s garage-ready.

This model is available in five finishes, but any colors other than white add to the overall cost. It also lacks both a freezer light and an ice maker, which means you’re going to have to fill and empty trays somewhat regularly.

But if you want to avoid an ice-making mess, you can buy an optional ice maker designed for this model, although it will occupy more than a third of your freezer space. (Or you could buy one of our favorite countertop nugget ice makers instead.)

This is one robust fridge for just $1,200. You get an impressive 27.4 cubic feet of total storage space in this full-depth Samsung stainless steel side-by-side, plus the interior ice maker is located in the door to create even more freezer shelf space.

Aside from being roomy, this fridge includes a raft of both cooling and convenience features normally found on more expensive models. For instance, it instantly lowers the temperature of your fridge and freezer section to quickly bring recently purchased goods to their appropriate storage levels.

The in-door water dispenser also dispenses both cubed and crushed ice, but it lacks a light, which makes it difficult to accurately fill a glass in a dark kitchen. There's an LCD panel on the inside of the fridge for monitoring refrigerator and freezer temperatures, ice maker filter status, and managing lock controls.

Multi-vent cooling lowers the temp in every corner of the fridge, which prevents ice-infused liquids. While it lacks some of the fancier frills of some of our other, more expensive choices, this Samsung provides a lot of storage space, especially in its freezer, for a relatively low price tag.

If it's even possible for a refrigerator to be entertaining, this French door LG does it and thankfully it doesn't skimp on functionality in the process. Right off the bat, you’ll notice a shaded window on the right door—just gently knock twice and it turns clear so you can take a peek inside. Then, grab the door just right, and a door within the main door opens with easy, quick access to snacks and drinks without letting all the cool air out from the main fridge compartment.

If you’re tired of old-fashioned ice cubes, freshen drinks with Craft Ice, an interior ice maker that fashions slow-melting spheric ice modules. The exterior water dispenser also makes both crushed and cubed ice, and the dispenser/ice maker mechanism inside the left door is thin enough to accommodate three door bins.

Unfortunately, the freezer is organized with deep, basket-style compartments, which makes it difficult to quickly find what you need in a pinch. But if you’re a frequent party host, the freezer really steps up the ice production, so you’re less likely to ever run out, even during a lively party.

There are side-by-side fridges with fancier features, but you'd be hard-pressed to find a better deal than this Whirlpool model, considering its combination of capacity, extras, aesthetic appeal, and overall cooling excellence.

This fridge has a spacious 28.7-cubic-foot capacity and is equipped with both an extra tall exterior water and ice dispenser to accommodate all sizes of glasses, bottles, and pitchers. The interior ice bin is installed on the door to free up freezer storage space. There's a good variety of glass shelves inside, and they extend wall-to-wall to truly maximize all fridge and freezer space available.

There is also a temperature management system that senses and adapts to keep food fresh. Special pantry and deli drawers maintain organization, too. If any foods have defrosted in between the grocery store and home, the freezer has a Fast Freeze option that quickly drops the temperature 10 degrees lower than the normal setting, so your foods can get back to fully frozen in a jiffy.

Unfortunately, if you’re looking to match this fridge with your kitchen decor, it's available only in a “stainless” finish.

This full-depth GE bottom freezer, which has been available since 2022, is still well-respected in its category, and it costs around $1,000 less than it was at first launch. Its enduring popularity can likely be attributed to its 24.9-cubic-foot capacity that’s generous for this class of fridge, although the water filter is unusually bulky and takes up a little more cooling space than we would have liked.

Fortunately, it includes an ice maker along with a larger-than-average storage bin—perfect for party preparation. A turbo cool setting automatically drops the internal temperature if the door’s been open too long. We also love the unique, horizontally-sliding snack drawer that easily moves across the fridge to make room for taller items below.

Other nice features include touch temperature controls, an open door alarm, and a fingerprint-resistant stainless finish. And, as found on most top- or bottom-freezer models, the top door is reversible, so you can install it to swing open left or right.

This model is also Energy Star-certified, meaning it meets strict standards set by the EPA and uses between 9 and 15 percent less energy than models that have not been rated.

Samsung makes arguably the smartest fridges around, particularly with its Family Hub models that integrate seamlessly into smart home ecosystems. The right door of this appliance boasts a 32-inch vertical touch screen that provides access to recipes, shopping lists, ordering, and other food-related tasks. In addition to these functions, it acts a streaming interface and display for your kids’ school drawings or home videos.

Inside, this fridge has a camera that recognizes and automatically labels 37 unobscured fresh food items such as fruits and vegetables, or you can do this manually. On the left side, there’s a double quick-access door. Behind that lives a beverage center designed with both a regular water dispenser and an auto-refill water pitcher with an infuser.

Open its doors either manually or automatically by touching a sensor. To better preserve different types of food, one of the freezer drawers lets you set one of five different temperatures depending on what you’re storing. A dual ice maker makes both standard cubes and smaller “ice bits,” but not crushed ice. An internal camera lets you see what’s inside your fridge—or not—when shopping via the smartphone app, which also lets you monitor the fridge’s power usage.

GE Profile appliances are considered high-end within the GE family, sitting above standard GE appliances but below its relatively expensive Monogram line. To put it simply, you get luxury features without a large price tag by investing in this model.

Inside the left of the four-door refrigerator is an auto-refillable water pitcher that doubles as a dispenser, and the bottom left freezer compartment contains roomy storage that holds a whopping 12.5 pounds of filtered ice. The bottom right can also function as a freezer, chiller, or fridge if you run out of space up top.

This fridge doesn't have an exterior water dispenser, but it does have a built-in pitcher with advanced filtration inside the left door. The door-in-door with rotating bin is a standout feature for us, too.

From a design POV, you get a smooth stainless steel exterior door finish with sleek handles, and the interior is highlighted by silver shelf and drawer trim handles, and an LED light wall. If you want to take advantage of smart features, the fridge has built-in WiFi and compatibility with the SmartHQ app for a fully customizable experience.

Each fridge form offers varying cubic-foot capacity, usually expressed as total space. Until recently, inside capacity was somewhat restricted by the need for refrigerant circulation and insulation inside a fridge’s thick walls. But many newer models with updated cooling and insulation technologies have slimmed these inside walls to create so-called “max,” “mega,” or “XL” capacity models, creating more inside capacity without increasing exterior dimensions.

Bear in mind, some vendors fudge on capacity, often rounding up to the nearest whole number in the name of the fridge. For instance, a “23” cubic foot model may actually only contain 22.5 cubic feet of storage.

So how do you decide how big your new fridge needs to be? Obviously, how much capacity you can buy depends on how much space you have in your kitchen for a refrigerator.

Figure 4- to 6 cubic feet per family member, leaning toward the higher end for a lot of shared items. Alternatively, you can start with ten cubic feet for the household, then add 1.5 cubic feet per household member to get a minimum capacity.

In terms of fridge-freezer split, most models assign 65 to 70 percent of space to cooling and the remaining 30 to 35 percent to freezing. If you’re a bulk buyer or frequent meal preparer, we recommend you find a model with more freezer space.

You want shelves and inside door bins to offer varying height adjustments to accommodate the tallest types of containers such as opened wine bottles. Some fridges offer shelves that slide out and door bins that can accommodate bulky gallon jugs or tilt out for easier access.

All refrigerators include storage drawers, but the best are those models that offer drawers with some sort of temperature and/or humidity control. When shopping, check the drawers to make sure they slide in and out smoothly, come out easily for cleaning, and don’t feel cheap.

Refrigerators with twin doors—side-by-side and French—are the only models in which you’ll find exterior in-door water and ice dispensers. Most in-door water dispensers also dispense ice—often both cubed and crushed—but some don’t offer ice, so double-check this before you buy. If a twin-door model doesn’t include an exterior water dispenser, there’s likely one inside the fridge.

If you’re replacing a fridge without an in-door water dispensing with one that does, you (or, more likely, a plumber) will likely first have to install a separate water line that connects from your under-sink pipes to your new refrigerator. You’ll also occasionally have to change the refrigerator’s water filter—usually around twice a year—but ask first to make sure of the timing. Our advice: order in a supply of filters so you’re sure you’re always dispensing the freshest filtered water.

A growing number of “smart” refrigerators include interior cameras to let you view your fridge’s contents to confirm if you need something while grocery shopping. A camera-equipped smart model with a smart screen on the door can also scan your remaining ingredients and create an edible recipe, assemble a shopping list and create an order from a grocery delivery service, and even identify what food items have reached their expiration date.

Yes, refrigerators still come in basic white, but you also can choose from a growing variety of subtleties such as gray and black tones in shiny or matte finishes (with smudge- or finger-resistant coatings). But a few fridge makers also offer custom-made colors, including finishes to match your kitchen cabinets or decor.

Do note that a model listed as having a “stainless” finish does not necessarily mean you’re getting an actual stainless steel door. In most instances, just the word “stainless” likely means the door is some other material, not stainless steel. To make sure you’re getting a true stainless steel door, first feel it, as stainless steel is cooler to the touch than other materials. Also, take along a refrigerator magnet—most stainless steel doors are not magnetic.

There are, essentially, only five forms of fridges, all defined by how the doors and drawers are arranged. In order of price, features available, and capacity (low to high), the five fridge forms are:

This is the most familiar no-frills fridge format with two doors: a top freezer compartment and a larger fresh food compartment below. Top freezer fridges are usually the least expensive fridge option, landing somewhere between $500-$900. They also range between 10 to 24 cubic feet of space, and are best suited for single folks or couples on a budget, or as a second fridge for a recreational basement or garage.

No frills means you’re unlikely to find any top freezer models with in-door water dispensers or an ice maker. Most models come with reversible doors that open left-to-right or right-to-left, however.

A.k.a. “bottom freezer” models, these refrigerators are equipped with a single door up top and a freezer drawer usually encompassing two shelving tiers below. Bottom freezer models are presumably more attractive to those with fewer freezer needs since you have to kneel or bend over to rummage through it.

Bottom freezer models generally provide a bit more freezer space than a top freezer— around 18 to 22 cubic feet of total capacity—and typically cost more than a top freezer model. Like a top freezer model, you’re unlikely to find bottom freezer models with in-door water dispensers, but some pricier versions do include ice makers.

Rather than a horizontal freezer, side-by-side models offer vertical freezing and cooling compartments. Side-by-side interior horizontal space, however, is narrower than a top- or bottom-freezer model, so wide platter trays or frozen pizzas may not fit. Side-by-sides are more likely to offer in-door water or ice dispensing, which can further reduce freezer space.

Essentially a side-by-side with a bottom freezer, except the two top French doors open out to reveal a single large and wide cooling compartment. Since there’s no wall dividing the top section, French door models offer the highest capacity—up to around 30 cubic feet worth of fresh food room—which makes these the best option for a large and active family.

French door models usually include either in-door or interior water and ice dispensers. Higher capacity, of course, means higher prices—French door models start at around $1,400 depending on interior space and features, such as more convenient separate dual freezer drawers.

Four-door French-door fridges replace the bottom freezer drawer(s) with freezer doors that open into separate compartments. However, you’ll see models with French doors with dual drawer freezers referred to as “four-door,” which they are not since a drawer is decidedly not a door. More expensive four-door models allow you to convert one of the freezer sections into an extra refrigerator compartment if you need extra cooling space.

Most refrigerators last around seven years. Spending a bit more now on a better model means you can go a lot longer without having to buy a replacement. Models with long warranties usually signal a longer expected lifespan.

Our general refrigerator shopping advice: you already know your current refrigerator’s shortcomings (that’s presumably why you’re looking to buy a new one). Match your known needs with the features outlined above that you find attractive before you go to a store and are confronted by a pushy salesperson.

It's not recommended to turn your fridge on and off for short periods of time because it can actually increase wear on the compressor, but powering down during longer absences helps save energy and prevents issues like mold.

Before heading out for vacation or an extended trip, you should remove all food, clean and dry the interior, unplug the appliance, and keep the doors propped open for air circulation.

We Tested Home Generators to Find the Best Picks for a Power Outage

📅 2021-02-15  |  Read Source →

By Glenda Taylor, Austin Fracchia, Mark Wolfe, Zach Lazzari

Updated Nov 3, 2026 9:44 PM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

During a power outage, a home generator can keep the lights on, the HVAC running, and the appliances powered, but there are a lot of options out there, from whole-house to quiet inverter models. Because we’re big on helping our readers navigate the array of products that make life easier, our team tackled the task of testing home generators.

These independent power sources range in output for a variety of needs. After researching more than 40 top models, we selected the highest-scoring models in a range of categories for hands-on testing. Our favorite was the Champion 7500-Watt Dual-Fuel Generator thanks to its versatile operation and multiple outlets.

Whether you’re looking for a generator to run an entire home or to power only a few essentials, you can find a top-notch one in this lineup. Keep reading to find out how each one performed and to learn more about what to consider when shopping for the best home generator for your needs.

Bottom line: Our comprehensive testing reveals top home generators for every need, from budget-friendly to whole-house solutions.

When shopping for a generator, it’s easy to become overwhelmed by the available options on the market. While the following models vary in fuel type, electrical load, and intended use, each one earned a spot on this lineup by excelling in real-world tests.

Our Ratings: Ease of Use 4.5/5; Noise 4/5; Portability 4/5; Power 5/5; Value 5/5

This Champion 7,500-watt dual-fuel generator delivers enough electricity to power up an off-grid jobsite or keep critical home circuits running through a utility outage. It includes numerous convenience features, like a push-button electric starter, covered outlets, and a fold-out transport handle. In our tests, the push-button starter worked perfectly, no matter the weather.

We appreciated its two locking 30A outlets (one 120V and one 120/240V) for powering a home, workshop, RV, or heavy-duty tools like portable welders. It also has four household-style 120V-20A outlets for lower-wattage applications. On a 7.7-gallon tank of gas, this generator supports a 50 percent load for up to 10 hours or up to 5.5 hours with a 20-pound propane cylinder. Using propane, we ran a portable table saw, miter saw, router, and air compressor for 4 hours and had just under half a tank of propane left over.

On the downside, its heavy weight made it tough to navigate across a yard and lift in and out of a truck. It also does not include an idle controller, which leads to more fuel consumption than eco-throttle-equipped generators. Still, it offered plenty of power and access points for the price.

Get the Champion 7500-watt home generator at Blain’s Farm & Fleet.

Our Ratings: Ease of Use 4/5; Power 4.5/5; Portability 5/5; Noise 4/5; Value 4.5/5

A no-frills dual-fuel generator that’s small enough to tuck away in the corner of a garage or in a truck bed, the Pulsar 2200-watt generator delivered an easy experience powering our test equipment. The 1.18-gallon generator can run for up to 8 hours on half load on a single tank of gas.

Setting up the generator for the first time was fairly straightforward, though filling the oil for the first time proved to be a challenge. The side panel that we needed to unscrew and pop off to access the oil fill port and dipstick was difficult to remove. We also had to lay the generator on its side to fill it with oil, which required a bit of balancing as it’s not completely flat. Balancing while pouring oil was a bit tricky, to say the least. Starting it was a piece of cake. Turning on the Pulsar requires pulling a recoil start, but the engine kicked on after just a couple of pulls—even in the cold.

We used several appliances, including a couple of heat guns and hair dryers at the same time to test the generator at different loads. As promised, the generator handled a combined 1,800 watts without much issue and shut power off when we overloaded it with 2,300 watts.

The Pulsar’s noise level seemed louder than expected, but it was still in a reasonable range for its output. It’s a great option for budget-priced backup power for light-duty power needs such as small DIY projects and powering a couple of household appliances during an outage.

Our Ratings: Ease of Use 5/5; Noise 4/5; Portability 4/5; Power 4.5/5; Value 4.5/5

It’s hard to beat Westinghouse’s 4,500-watt inverter generator for power output, versatility, and portability. It’s not the highest-wattage model we tested, but it excelled at running several power tools, including grinders and saws, as well as the essentials in our travel trailer. Setting it up was a snap—all we had to do was add oil and fill the gas tank. Best of all, it’s quiet and efficient.

While this would be a great emergency generator, we also think it’s a top-notch pick for RVs, camping, and remote powering due to its quiet and efficient operation.

The iGen4500 has a 3.4-gallon gasoline tank that can run up to 18 hours. Since it’s an inverter, it only burns enough to cover the appliances or tools it’s powering. This makes it more eco-friendly than noninverter models that burn fuel at a set rate no matter how much power is being drawn. This backup generator offers a variety of power ports, including a 30A port, two 20A ports, and two USB ports. However, it weighs almost 105 pounds without fuel, so lifting it up and down can be challenging for one person.

Read our full review: Westinghouse iGen4500 Inverter Generator

Get the Westinghouse home generator at Amazon, Tractor Supply Co., or Walmart.

Our Ratings: Ease of Use 5/5; Noise 4/5; Portability 4/5; Power 5/5; Value 4/5

The Honda EU2200i inverter generator offers reliability, simple operation, and low noise. Honda estimates that the EU2200i runs at 48 to 57 weighted decibels, and we found that its sound easily gets lost in the background. It comes equipped with a Honda GXR120 commercial series engine that delivers 120 volts and up to 1,800 running watts and 2,200 starting watts. A 125V-20A duplex outlet allows users to plug in two electrical cords without worrying about overloading the generator. T

his machine is compatible with the Honda My Generator mobile app for remote monitoring and is parallel-ready for use with a second Honda EU2200i (connection kit sold separately). It also features a built-in carbon monoxide sensor and safety shutoff. In two separate tests, we used the Honda generator to power a full-size refrigerator for 4 hours and then a portable table saw and miter saw for an 8-hour workday. We even added a USB hub to charge a phone and laptop.

In each test, the devices worked perfectly, the electrical current remained strong and steady, and the breaker didn’t trip. The generator’s molded plastic handle is well placed, and when combined with the machine’s proportions, the unit is fairly easy to carry, even though it weighs nearly 50 pounds. We would have appreciated a built-in USB port in addition to the wall-style outlet, though. As it was, we had to unplug a tool that draws 10A just to charge a device using less than 3A.

Still, a clean electrical current and quiet operation in a lightweight, easy-to-stow package make the Honda an excellent choice for a home backup generator.

Get the Honda home generator at Amazon or Northern Tool + Equipment.

Our Ratings: Ease of Use 4.5/5; Power 5/5; Portability 4/5; Noise 4/5; Value 4.5/5

Westinghouse WGen9500DF is an all-around top pick when the power goes out. It has a 6.6-gallon gasoline tank that will keep it running for 12 hours at 25 percent load and up to 8 hours at 50 percent load. (Actual tank runtime may vary depending on gasoline quality and electrical load.) With a 20-pound propane tank, the generator will run up to 7 hours at 50 percent load.

This is a heavy model, weighing 211 pounds, but it mainly came assembled—all we had to do was attach the wheels and the handle. We tested it first with gasoline, followed by propane, and the results were excellent with both fuels. We ran several power tools simultaneously and powered an oven/range and a clothes dryer (at separate times). A nice perk was the remote fob, which allowed us to start the generator from 90 feet away.

The WGen9500DF is a good pick for those who want an emergency generator to power more than just a couple of cell phones.

Get the Westinghouse home generator at Amazon.

Our Ratings: Ease of Use 5/5; Noise 5/5; Portability 3.5/5; Power 4.5/5; Value 5/5

A distinct downside with fuel-powered generators is the inability to run them indoors due to hazardous fumes. Power stations, however, can be used indoors to power several appliances and electronic items, depending on their capacity. The Generac GB2000 is a beefy power backup that provides up to 1,600 running watts of electricity and comes with three standard outlets, four USB ports, and a 12V DC car port. Charging the GB2000 took about 3.5 hours when we plugged it directly into a standard AC outlet.

We also charged it with solar panels, but the day was slightly overcast, which kept it from charging quickly—it took about 5 hours to charge from 28 to 100 percent. A sunnier day or stronger solar panels would likely have charged it more quickly. When fully charged, we used it to run our fridge, a miter saw, lamps, a laptop, and cell phone—not all simultaneously. We made about 20 cuts with the miter saw and noticed no lag in the saw’s power. It took a couple of hours to fully charge the cell phone by placing it on the wireless charging panel at the top of the GB2000.

We left the fridge and laptop plugged in for several hours without depleting the battery—we still had 20 percent when we unplugged them. The Generac is well suited as an emergency backup for essential appliances or a remote power source for tailgating and camping, but at 43 pounds, it’s a bit heavy if you plan to carry it very far on trails.

Get the Generac home generator at The Home Depot or Walmart.

Before testing, we carefully researched more than 40 portable and standby generators and over a dozen high-capacity power stations. We looked at consumer satisfaction, ease of use, price, and versatility. We also considered brand reputation—manufacturers like Champion, Honda, Westinghouse, and Briggs & Stratton are highly respected in the generator industry.

Our goal was to pinpoint models that would suit diverse user needs. This list includes powerful, gas-fueled, and dual-fueled generators in addition to quieter inverters and even solar power stations.

Team members assembled and fueled the generators using the appropriate fuel types. Then we plugged in power tools, home appliances, and electronic devices to see if the generators would produce the advertised wattage. We used an online wattage calculator to help us estimate the correct wattage for testing. In addition, we took note of the noise the generators produced when operating (some models are quieter than others), and we took portability into account.

For each generator, we awarded points based on quality and performance. At the end of testing, we averaged the points to determine which models were best suited to specific applications and users.

Several factors can affect each type of generator’s overall functionality when the power goes out. Keep the following considerations in mind when searching for the best home generator to keep you powered up when severe weather or a natural disaster causes an outage.

Finding the best generator for your power needs involves weighing the four types available. Each type has its own set of pros and cons, and one is likely to suit your home’s specific needs better than the others.

Home standby generators, also called whole-house generators, are large, permanently installed generators that connect to a home’s electrical panel for backup power during an electrical outage. They are equipped with an automatic transfer switch (explained in later sections) that automatically switches on the generator’s power supply when it detects a loss of utility power. Home standby generators are most commonly powered by natural gas or propane, but some diesel models are available.

Home standby generators have the largest power ratings and are usually, though not always, powerful enough to satisfy the electrical demands of an entire home. They can power utilities such as water heaters and central air conditioners. The downside is that they’re the most expensive to purchase and install and require installation by certified professionals. An electrician is needed to connect it to the electrical panel, and a plumber may be required to connect the propane or natural gas line to the generator.

As the name suggests, portable generators are small and can be moved to different locations. Compared to standby options, these are cheap home generators. They can have a wide range of power output ratings. Some may only power a limited number of electrical items and appliances, while large-capacity portable generators may have a 30A outlet for connecting a transfer switch.

Many options are gas-powered generators, but some are powered by diesel or propane. Multifuel generators (dual- and tri-fuel) are powered by multiple fuel sources, including gasoline, diesel, propane, and/or natural gas. Be advised that fuel-powered generators should never be used indoors since the engine can emit a toxic level of CO, leading to sickness or death.

Inverter generators are technically a type of portable generator, but they offer a different set of pros and cons. As opposed to conventional generator engines that constantly operate at the same output rate, inverter generators adapt to power demand.

This makes them more fuel efficient, allowing them to have smaller fuel tanks and more portability than other generators. This efficiency reduces their CO emissions. It also makes them quiet generators, usually only producing around a 60-decibel noise level—the same volume level as a typical conversation.

All of these advantages make them a popular choice among campers and RVers who value silent operation and portability. The disadvantages? Inverter generators are typically more expensive than regular portable generators and generally produce less power.

Portable power stations are essentially battery banks that store electrical power. Instead of using gasoline, diesel, propane, or natural gas, power stations are charged in a standard electrical outlet or via solar panels. Solar-powered power stations are often called solar generators and offer a practically unlimited and renewable power source—just as long as the sun is shining.

These power stations are usually designed to be compact and lightweight for incredible portability. They are also virtually silent when in operation. In addition, they can be safely used indoors since they don’t produce any harmful emissions.

The primary disadvantages are that they’re usually more expensive than conventional portable generators and have significantly lower power output. That being said, they can be used to power a handful of essential appliances during an outage.

A generator’s size equates to the amount of power in watts it can generate, ranging from 800 to greater than 50,000-watt power capacity. Generators are sized in both “peak watts” (also called “starting watts”) and “running watts,” but the running watts rating reveals how many continuous watts the generator can supply to keep the electricity running to the connected items. Peak watts are the number of watts the generator can produce for a few seconds to satisfy the extra demand from starting motor-driven items like power tools.

The running watts rating dictates the number of electrical appliances you can run simultaneously and for how long. Because a generator’s starting watts can be 1,000 to 2,000 watts greater than its running watts, it’s important to understand the difference when determining your home’s power requirements.

To determine the generator size you need, calculate the total number of watts your most essential electrical items consume. All electrical appliances are required to have a label that displays their wattage rating; you simply need to add together the wattage figure listed on each item you want to power during an electrical outage.

Generally speaking, a refrigerator consumes between 700 and 1,200 watts; a window air conditioner consumes around 1,000 watts; a portable space heater consumes about 1,500 watts; and lights can be anywhere between 60 and 600 watts. Most households typically require around 5,000 watts to power the most essential components.

A transfer switch connects a generator to a home’s main electrical panel and allows you to switch, or “transfer,” between grid power and generator power. There are both manual and automatic transfer switches. Manual transfer switches require you to physically switch over the power at your electrical panel when the power goes out, while an automatic transfer switch automatically switches over power from the grid to generator power when it detects a loss of power.

Generators can be equipped with various features that make their operation as convenient as possible. These include:

Along with the various features that contribute to convenience, there are also a few important safety features that many generators incorporate into their design, particularly with regard to CO detection. These include:

Operating a generator isn’t complicated, but there are a few tips you’ll want to keep in mind to ensure effective and safe operation and avoid any harm to yourself or your generator when the power goes out.

First of all, it’s important to use extension cords that are sufficiently powerful. As a general rule, use at least 12-gauge outdoor-rated electrical cords for larger appliances (like refrigerators) and at least 14-gauge cords for everything else. Cord length is also a concern since the voltage drop from cords that are too long can damage or destroy the appliances they’re supplying. Although you’ll want to keep a portable generator outside and away from your home, remember not to exceed a 100-foot extension cord length.

When running a home generator, it’s best to keep it on a level surface. If a generator is placed on a slope or hill, the internal lubrication system could malfunction and lead to premature engine wear and damage. Finally, ensure the generator has cooled down before you refill it since spilling gasoline on a hot engine can cause a fire.

Since generators can be both lifesaving and expensive, it’s natural to have some questions before you’re confident in your decision. To this end, consider the answers to these frequently asked questions. Together with the above shopping considerations, these answers provide you with just about everything you need to know about emergency power generators.

Most municipalities and towns require that you have a permit before a standby generator is installed. However, not all locations have this requirement. Check with your local zoning authority to find out if a permit is required in your community.

To determine the necessary power rating for your generator, add up the wattage of all the appliances you want to run during an electrical outage. As a general rule, a generator should have at least a 5,000-watt capacity to power a home’s essential appliances. But, your home could require substantially more power than that.

Yes, fuel-powered home generators require oil to operate properly without wearing out the moving parts.

Most manufacturers recommend that the whole-house generator be installed at least 5 feet from doors, windows, vents, and any flammable material. There may also be local regulations about the placement of a whole-house generator, so make sure to check local guidelines to ensure that your whole-house generator meets local building codes and installation guidelines.

No. A generator should not be plugged into a house outlet because it is both dangerous and illegal in many locations. This is called backfeeding and it can severely damage the home’s electrical system or even cause a fire. Additionally, any technicians who are working on electrical lines on the same power grid are at increased risk of electrocution.

You can connect a generator to individual appliances using a series of extension cords, or if you want direct power for entire circuits in the home, have a standby generator professionally installed.

On average, it will take an experienced technician about 1 to 2 full days to complete the installation of your whole-house generator. It could take 1 to 2 weeks to prep for the installation, such as pouring a concrete pad and running the wires connecting the generator to the electrical panel.

The exact lifespan depends on the specific product, but in general, the overall lifespan of a whole-house generator is about 1,500 to 3,000 hours.

How to shop like a pro during Black Friday and Cyber Monday

📅 2020-11-21  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The annual deal extravaganza can be overwhelming, but the right tool and a little patience can help you score big.

The annual deal extravaganza can be overwhelming, but the right tool and a little patience can help you score big.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If you buy something from a link, The Verge may earn a commission. See our ethics statement.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Despite Amazon and other retailers filling the summer and fall months with more sales than ever before, Black Friday and Cyber Monday remain the biggest shopping days of the year. And, in recent years, the sale periods have started earlier, giving you more time to take advantage of the deals.

We’re already hard at work to find the best prices on phones, accessories, 4K TVs, noise-canceling headphones, gaming gear, and other tech throughout the deal-packed weeks culminating in the big events, whether you plan to shop in person or online.

You can always count on us to publish only the best deals that you need to know about, no matter the season. However, if you want to be among the first people to spot price drops on the products you’re interested in, you’ll want to get a head start on your Black Friday and Cyber Monday game plan. Thankfully, we have a few suggestions for scouting early sales at your favorite retailers, finding the right price-tracking tool, and setting up email alerts to ensure you don’t miss rare discounts at Amazon and elsewhere.

The most effective way to find a truly great deal is to set up a price-tracking tool. We’ve included a few of our favorites below, many of which we use daily here at The Verge. Each can help you track deals at various retailers — including Amazon, Walmart, Best Buy, and Target — but they each do so differently and come with their own set of advantages / disadvantages.

All of them are free to use, and once you’ve added the products you’re interested in keeping an eye on, you can rest easy knowing that you don’t need to take any action until it comes time to make a purchase. Just make sure you keep your email notifications on so you see the alerts as they come in.

CamelCamelCamel is arguably the most popular price-tracking tool at The Verge, and for good reason: it’s incredibly useful since it tracks the price of every product sold on Amazon and can send you alerts when an item falls to a customizable price threshold. Once a product reaches the desired price you’ve set (or lower), you’ll get an email about it. You can use the extension’s price graph to determine what price is likely to happen based on historical data, but it’s always a shot in the dark.

To track prices and get alerts via email, you’ll need to create a free CamelCamelCamel account. Additionally, we suggest installing the site’s browser extension, The Camelizer, which works with Google Chrome, Safari, Mozilla Firefox, Opera, and Microsoft Edge. The handy tool lets you view pricing trends on a product-by-product basis and set your desired price without leaving Amazon. It’s fantastic, easy to use, and works in several countries, including the US, UK, Canada, Germany, Spain, France, Italy, and Australia.

As mentioned, the tracker only works for products listed on Amazon. However, in May, the company released CamelMart, its price tracker for Walmart. While it doesn’t have a browser extension (yet), its functionality is similar to CamelCamelCamel in that you can set price alerts and email notifications, so you’ll always know when your desired price threshold for a particular product is met.

Like CamelCamelCamel, Keepa is another handy tool for tracking product prices on Amazon. Keepa has charts that track pricing trends and can alert you when it finds a deal. Similarly, you can also import any of your Amazon wishlists.

Unlike CamelCamelCamel, however, you don’t necessarily need to create an account to track prices with Keepa; just install the appropriate browser extension for Chrome, Microsoft Edge, Firefox, or Opera, and Keepa will automatically add a price history graph to supported product listings. The embedded graph shows pricing history and allows you to specify various pricing details. If you set a desired price, Keepa will send you a price alert via email, push notification, Telegram, or RSS.

Slickdeals aggregates some of the best deals around the internet, as discovered by its team and community of users. Its site also lets you browse coupons and create deal alerts based on specific keywords, though the latter works a little differently on Slickdeals than it does with the sites above.

You can type in a product name, like “Nintendo Switch 2,” “OLED TVs,” or a retailer’s name, and once it’s added to your list, you’ll get a deal alert if it meets your criteria. You can set it to alert you to literally any deal related to your keyword, or you can filter out deals so you’ll only be notified if, for example, the deal is popular enough to make it to Slickdeals’ front page or earns a high rating from the community. If you like browsing deals, it’s generally a good idea to visit Slickdeals frequently, especially since it usually has a Black Friday component that allows you to browse circulars for Best Buy, Target, and other retailers in advance.

Also, it’s just fun to see what the community digs up.

For a more hands-off way to see whether you’re getting a good deal, try your browser’s built-in tools. For a few years now, Edge has been able to tell you when you’re looking at the best price for a particular product from a range of retailers, and Google Chrome can track price fluctuations and notify you when a particular product goes on sale. Edge and Chrome are available on Windows, macOS, and Linux, as well as Android and iOS.

You may have already noticed that sometimes, when you’re shopping on Amazon, a sidebar opens to reveal more information about the product you’re looking at. Even if you haven’t used it, you can’t miss it; it’s prominently featured in Amazon’s upper nav bar and accessible by clicking the Price History button on a given listing.

Whether you’re using the Amazon app or the website, it’s a capable tool that, at its core, does some of the same tasks as our other recommendations. It can send price alerts and show up to a year’s worth of pricing data, helping you make informed buying decisions for products on Amazon. It can also recommend alternatives that might be a better value and, if you’ll allow it, can even automatically purchase products on your behalf.

It can do a lot more, too. For instance, you can have it update you when, say, a specific product launches, and it can shop on your behalf by adding specified items to your cart at a specified frequency. You can also use it to compare products sold on Amazon, and even upload photos of wishlists to find matching products.

If you have an Alexa-compatible smart gadget, such as an older Echo Dot Max or one of the company’s newer Echo devices, you can ask the virtual assistant to alert you when specific products go on sale. You can receive alerts up to 24 hours in advance of planned price drops for eligible products, or as soon as it notices a discount on anything saved in your shopping cart or wish list. If you say, “Alexa, buy it for me,” after being notified of the deal, the assistant will purchase the item for you once the discount becomes available.

To enable these features, open the Alexa app on your smartphone and navigate to More > Settings > Notifications > Amazon Shopping. Then, toggle the slider next to Deal Recommendations within the Shopping Recommendations section. Alexa will audibly notify you of deals moving forward, but if you happen to miss one, you can check for the notification on your phone or see whether you have any new notifications on an Echo device by checking for the yellow light. If it’s lit, just say, “Alexa, play notifications.”

You can receive similar notifications on your smartphone from the Amazon Shopping app, too. Just open the app and go to Settings > Notifications > Deals and recommendations, and toggle the slider next to the Savings option.

Keeping an eye on preferred retailers before Black Friday will help save you time (and money). Several major retailers have already detailed their plans in the run-up to the holidays, and many will offer early opportunities to save. For example:

You can expect more major retailers to detail their Black Friday plans shortly, so keep an eye on their respective websites and start filling your calendar accordingly.

Doorbusters, lightning deals, and other limited-time discounts are a tried-and-true hallmark of any Black Friday sale. They’ll usually be the most buzzworthy deals you’ll see all year, with truly low prices to spark impulsive spending. As you’re checking ads, make a note of these deals and read all of the fine print surrounding their availability. Some may require you to sign up in advance or get in line early (ahem, Amazon), and some can be so scarce that you won’t have the entire weekend — or even an hour — to claim them.

Although many stores will automatically match their competitors’ pricing, sometimes you may need to twist their arm. It’s helpful to know the various price-matching policies of each retailer in the event that your preferred store runs out of stock on certain goods. You can get up to speed with our price-matching guide, which details the lengths each retailer will or won’t go to win your dollar. Unfortunately, some retailers put a temporary halt on their usual policies during special shopping events like Black Friday, but it never hurts to ask.

We’re a discerning bunch here at The Verge, so don’t fret if you’re going into Black Friday and Cyber Monday without any prep. We’ll be corralling the best tech deals and keeping our coverage up to date with new sale items while dropping old ones to avoid disappointment. While we usually suggest paying attention to deals starting the day after Thanksgiving — or November 27th, in this case — the truth is that several retailers typically jump the gun and start offering some great deals in the lead-up to the shopping holiday.

7 Best Early Black Friday Mattress Deals, Vetted by AD (2026)

📅 2020-11-18  |  Read Source →

Black Friday isn't until November 27, but that hasn't stopped mattress brands from getting a head start on the holiday savings. If your current mattress has seen better days or you're waking up with more aches than you went to bed with, there's no need to wait until a designated shopping day to get a great deal on an upgrade. Savings are available right now.

Brands like Saatva, Casper, Purple, and Birch are already offering early discounts on everything from hotel-worthy innerspring mattresses to cooling hybrids and organic options. Whether you're a side sleeper in need of a little extra cushioning for your hips and shoulders or someone who prefers a firmer, more supportive surface, there's something worth considering before the holiday rush kicks into high gear.

Below, you'll find seven AD-tested mattresses, complete with reviewer insights, standout features, and details about the current promotion. Get ready to find your next bed without losing sleep over the price tag.

Saatva is giving shoppers who have “luxury mattress” on their Black Friday wish list a head start on savings. The brand is taking over $400 off its popular Classic queen. Designed to deliver hotel-worthy comfort at home, the innerspring mattress features a dual-coil construction; a plush, three-inch Euro pillow top; and targeted lumbar support that helps promote spinal alignment. It also comes in three firmness options—plush soft, luxury firm, and firm—making it a versatile choice for different sleep positions. Reviewer Erin Kaplan, a combination side-and-stomach sleeper, praises the mattress's supportive feel, durability, and temperature regulation. “Overall, I get a better night’s sleep on a more consistent basis since I switched mattresses,” she says. Complimentary white-glove delivery, a 365-night sleep trial, and a lifetime warranty round out the perks of this high-end buy.

Selecting a firmer mattress doesn't mean you’re opting for less comfort. That’s especially true with the original PurpleFlex bed-in-a-box, currently $100 off ahead of Black Friday. Unlike traditional memory foam beds, the PurpleFlex features a two-inch GelFlex Grid designed to relieve pressure while maintaining a supportive, responsive feel. Beneath that layer, a durable foam base provides stability, making the eight-inch mattress especially appealing to back and stomach sleepers who prefer sleeping on top of their bed rather than sinking into it. AD tester Chapin Rockwell particularly likes the firm construction, reporting “less back pain and stiffness” after switching to the PurpleFlex.

We’ve all been there: You toss and turn, flip your pillow looking for cool relief, and feel disappointed when you find nothing of the sort. If you tend to overheat easily, Casper's early Black Friday sale could be your chance to finally get a less sweaty night's sleep. Designed to keep nighttime overheating at bay, the mattress uses Casper's Snow Technology, which combines a pleasantly crisp QuickCool cover, phase-change material designed to absorb and disperse heat, and aluminum graphite HeatDelete bands that help draw excess heat away from the body. The mattress’s zoned memory foam and supportive springs also provide cushioning and spinal support without the overly plush sensation of an all-foam mattress. After testing the Snow Hybrid, contributor Kate McGregor describes its feel as "squishy when I lay down," while noting that the foam doesn't make getting out of bed difficult. She also loves its motion isolation and cooling qualities, although there were some lingering off-gassing smells after unboxing. Really, the only hot thing about this bed is its early Black Friday Deal: the sale offers over $800 off the queen-sized hybrid mattress, making now a great time to buy.

To make your bedroom a little more environmentally conscious, consider starting with the Birch Natural Mattress, a medium-firm hybrid model that’s free of polyurethane foam, fiberglass, formaldehyde, and pesticides. Instead, it’s made from natural and organic materials, including breathable organic cotton, natural wool, and organic latex that’s certified by the Global Organic Latex Standard (GOLS), breathable organic cotton, and natural wool. Individually wrapped steel coils provide responsive support, while the latex offers gentle contouring (as opposed to the sinking feeling you might get from memory foam). Contributor Zoë Sessums was particularly impressed by the mattress's balance of softness and firmness, calling its feel "Goldilocks perfection." Birch Natural also features reinforced sides for additional stability that you’ll enjoy as you sit on the edge of your bed to put on your shoes. Free shipping, a 365-night sleep trial, and a limited lifetime warranty add even more appeal to the brand’s early holiday sale, which takes 25% off sitewide, bringing the queen-size Birch Natural Mattress down to $1,399 from $1,865.

The WinkBed is a luxury hybrid mattress that caters to your preferred sleeping style, and it’s currently 30% off as part of the brand’s Veterans Day sale. What makes the WinkBeds particularly versatile is its four firmness options: Softer, Luxury Firm, Firmer, and Plus (the last level, Plus, is designed for sleepers who need additional support). Beneath its plush yet supportive Euro pillow top, pressure-relieving gel foam and individually wrapped coils work together to provide cushioning, targeted support, and motion isolation. A moisture-wicking Tencel cover also helps promote a cooler sleeping environment. But don’t just take WinkBeds’ word for it. Contributor Mark Burka notes that the mattress "provides strong edge support and wicks away heat," two features that help distinguish it from other hybrids. Moreover, it also comes with free shipping, a 365-night sleep trial, and a lifetime warranty, offering plenty of time to determine whether it's the right fit for you.

Side sleepers searching for a more supportive night's rest should put shopping Nolah's early Black Friday sale at the top of their shopping list. Shopping editor Audrey Lee, who tested an earlier iteration of the Evolution, praises its high level of comfort and cooling abilities. “I found this design’s contouring quality top-tier for pressure relief, but I still had ample support thanks to the coils,” she says. The brand is currently taking 35% off its mattresses, bringing the queen-size Evolution from $2,277 down to $1,480. Designed to cushion sensitive pressure points without sacrificing support, the luxury hybrid combines responsive AirFoam Luxe, individually wrapped coils, and targeted lumbar support to help keep the spine aligned. Shoppers can choose among Plush, Luxury Firm, and Firm options, all of which can accommodate an optional GlacioTex cooling pillow-top upgrade for additional cushioning and temperature regulation. A 365-night sleep trial, free shipping, and a limited lifetime warranty make the early holiday deal even sweeter.

Handcrafted in Illinois, this mattress combines three inches of GOLS-certified organic Dunlop latex for a breathable, responsive sleeping surface with organic cotton and wool that’s certified by the Global Organic Textile Standard (GOTS). (That’s a third party verification that ensures the organic origin and processing of the materials used in the bed.) The Natural Escape’s individually pocketed coil system features seven zones of targeted lumbar support, making the mattress a strong contender for shoppers who prefer a firmer surface. "I feel super supported," notes contributor Kristi Kellogg of her experience testing the Natural Escape. She also has zero back pain while sleeping on it and appreciates the straightforward delivery and setup process: It arrives compressed in a box and includes free shipping and returns, a 365-night sleep trial, and a 20-year warranty. Need another reason to consider making the switch? My Green Mattress is currently taking 10% off orders during its Go Green Sale, with the Natural Escape Organic Mattress queen going for $1,829, down from $2,079.

Here are the price-matching policies for Best Buy, Walmart, and others

📅 2020-11-17  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

With Black Friday approaching, we’re here to let you know which retailers will go the extra mile on your behalf.

With Black Friday approaching, we’re here to let you know which retailers will go the extra mile on your behalf.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If you buy something from a link, The Verge may earn a commission. See our ethics statement.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Nothing is more frustrating than buying a new pair of headphones or an OLED TV, only to find out you could have gotten it cheaper somewhere else a few days or weeks later. That’s why, to keep customers happy and prevent them from shopping elsewhere, some retailers offer price-matching policies that promise to match a lower price found elsewhere. That kind of information comes in handy year-round, but it’s especially helpful during the weeks of deals leading up to Black Friday and Cyber Monday.

Below are the price-matching policies for several major retailers. A few things to note: First, each retailer has its own set of rules and restrictions. We’ve summarized some of them here, but we recommend visiting the provided links and reading the policies carefully. Second, most retailers that serve as marketplaces for other sellers, including wholesale retailers like Costco, don’t offer protections, and many traditional stores won’t match their prices.

Lastly, there are always exceptions to the rules. If you’re looking for a deal on a product and you know it’s available for a lower price elsewhere (or it was previously offered at the same retailer), it never hurts to call or chat with a customer service rep and ask if they’ll match the price. You have nothing to lose but time, and there’s always a chance they may find a loophole or make a special exception.

Amazon doesn’t offer price matching. Full stop. You can contact customer support, but even if you’re an Amazon Prime customer, they likely won’t help. That said, the online retailer does offer a liberal return policy, meaning you can often return an item you purchased from Amazon if it’s available for less elsewhere (or at Amazon). Then, you can just repurchase the product wherever it’s cheapest.

Target used to let shoppers price match identical items sold by select retailers, including Amazon, but no longer does so. Under its current policy, shoppers can only request a price adjustment if they find an identical qualifying item for less than they paid on Target.com, at their local Target store, or through an eligible Target Circle deal. Target will also price match Target Plus Partner (the retailer’s name for third-party sellers) items if the same Target Plus Partner on Target.com has it for less, or with a Target Circle deal. Price matches can be requested at the time of purchase or within 14 days after purchase.

For the 2026 holiday season, Target has extended the aforementioned price-match window. If you purchase an eligible item between November 1st and December 24th, you can request a price adjustment if its price drops at Target during that period. The extended policy also applies to eligible Target Plus Partner purchases.

To request a price match, you’ll need to show the online price or digital version of the ad and provide your original receipt or packing slip; screenshots and photos aren’t accepted. Alternatively, you can call for online purchases at 1-800-591-3869 or chat online via contactus.target.com.

However, note that Target’s policy excludes in-store price matches from other Target stores. It also can’t be combined with Target Circle Bonuses, Target coupons, Registry coupons, or gift card promotions, and doesn’t apply to clearance, closeout, liquidation, damaged, used, open-box, refurbished, pre-owned, or rent-to-own items. In addition, Target won’t match prices between items sold directly by Target and those sold by third-party Target Plus Partner sellers.

Best Buy’s Price Match Guarantee states that a product could be eligible for a price reduction if it’s new, identical to the competitor’s product, immediately available at a qualifying competitor’s store or on its site, and not shown on Best Buy’s exclusion list — that includes “items for sale the Thursday before Thanksgiving Day through the Monday after Thanksgiving.” Items must have the identical model number and color. However, Best Buy only matches prices that are available to all customers and does not match membership-exclusive, loyalty-program, or paid-member discounts; thus, it excludes events like Amazon Prime Day.

To get the difference for online purchases, you have to contact Best Buy via its on-site chat and provide the ad or website with the lower price. Alternatively, you can call 1-888-237-8289 or visit any Best Buy or Pacific Sales store. Approved retailers include Amazon, B&H Photo, Apple, Costco, Sam’s Club, Target, and Walmart. Items must be sold directly through the store, too, not a third-party seller.

Best Buy will also, upon request, match an in-store or online price that was lowered during the return and exchange period, which typically lasts up to 15 days. Keep in mind that the return window on most products is 60 days for both Best Buy Plus and My Best Buy Total members. For the holiday season, however, most eligible products purchased between October 30th and December 31st can be price matched through January 15th, 2027, if Best Buy lowers its own price. This policy doesn’t apply to competitors’ prices or certain products excluded from the holiday return period, including items that require a third-party contract, like smartphones.

As of now, Walmart doesn’t price-match competitors, though Walmart stores will match prices on identical items sold at Walmart.com provided they’re in stock online.

However, the policy doesn’t apply to residents of Alaska, Hawaii, or Puerto Rico. Additionally, it doesn’t apply to special event prices, like those seen during Black Friday. Walmart also doesn’t retroactively price-match items purchased on Walmart.com that drop in price after the time of purchase. The retailer doesn’t price-match items purchased from Walmart Marketplace retailers or third-party sellers, either, and Walmart says the store manager on duty makes the final decision when you request a price match.

Apple doesn’t officially advertise a price-matching policy on its website. That said, if Apple reduces the price of an Apple-branded product within 14 days of when you receive it, you can visit an Apple Store or contact Apple Support at 1-800-MY-APPLE within 14 days of the price change to request a refund or credit for the difference. However, this doesn’t apply to special promotional pricing, such as Black Friday or Cyber Monday deals.

B&H says it may honor a current lower price or match a competitor’s price, but only on a case-by-case basis. You must contact its customer service team via on-site chat, email, or by calling 1-800-606-6969 / 1-212-444-6615. Then, if approved, B&H Photo will price match one identical item per customer.

Costco does not price-match competitors. However, according to its website, it will issue a credit for the difference between the price a member paid and the promotional price if the item drops in price within 30 days of purchase. For promotions with specific sale dates, such as holiday savings events, requests must be submitted while the promotion is active and within 30 days of purchase. If you bought the product at a Costco warehouse location, visit the membership counter at the store where you made the purchase. Costco doesn’t price-match items purchased at Costco warehouses with items purchased on Costco.com.

Dell will price-match identical products from Dell, Amazon, Best Buy, Walmart, Target, HP, Apple, Lenovo, and several other retailers. Dell will not match prices available at Costco or Sam’s Club. You can call 1-877-275-3355 or chat online to run a price check, but you’ll need to provide a link to the competitor’s product. Dell outlines the specs you’ll need to compare to determine whether an item is eligible for a price match before you contact them, and before you make a purchase.

Normally, you can request a price adjustment within 30 days of purchase. For the holidays, Dell has extended that window for eligible PCs, monitors, and accessories purchased on Dell.com between November 1st and December 29th. You can request a price adjustment through January 15th, 2027, or within 30 days of the invoice date, whichever is later.

eBay doesn’t offer price matching on purchases made during the week of Thanksgiving through Cyber Monday, but it does provide a generous price guarantee on select items outside that window. If you buy an item marked with the “Best Price Guarantee” badge — or one featured in eBay Deals — and find a lower price from an approved competitor within 48 hours, eBay will give you 110 percent of the price difference in the form of a coupon. That means you’ll receive a coupon worth the full difference plus an extra 10 percent, which you can use toward your next eBay purchase. Approved competitors include Amazon, Best Buy, Target, and Walmart.

The products must be new, unopened, and identical to the competitor’s item. You must contact eBay Customer Service, and if they verify your claims are legitimate, you’ll receive a coupon that’s good toward your next eBay purchase. However, you can only price-match one item per day.

GameStop claims its website will automatically match competitor pricing to offer shoppers the best available price. However, according to its official policy, the retailer doesn’t offer additional price matching beyond the prices already displayed on its website.

If you make a request within the standard window or the holiday return period, Google will match online pricing from select retailers — specifically Amazon, Best Buy, Walmart, B&H Photo, Signifi, and Target. It doesn’t, however, match preorder pricing from other retailers or carrier deals. The product must also be in stock and identical, right down to the color and configuration. You also need to provide a public link to the promo page or product listing to verify the lower price, and the item must be in stock. Items sold by third-party sellers are ineligible for a price match.

Amazon Prime deals are eligible for price matching, but other members-only discounts aren’t. Other exclusions apply, too, including wholesale and educational pricing; mail-in offers and coupons; product bundles; clearance, open-box, refurbished, and pre-owned items; and products sold by third-party sellers on a competitor’s website. Google will only provide a price match on smartphones and other products with data plans if they’re unlocked devices.

You may also receive the difference if the product you purchased from the Google Store drops in price within the standard or holiday return period, though you’ll need to contact Google directly to get the price match or correction.

The Home Depot will match the price of identical, in-stock products from other retailers, except for membership-based wholesalers. The retailer must be able to ship the item to your location, so prices from regional retailers are ineligible. The price-match policy does not apply to special events like Black Friday, clearance prices, used and refurbished items, or rebate offers. You may request a price match within 30 days of your purchase.

You must bring the ad or listing to the register in-store. (You can also request price matches for online purchases that are shipped to the customer.) For online orders, you’ll need to provide the exact SKU for the item on both the competitor’s and The Home Depot’s sites. To request a price match by phone, you can call 1-800-430-3376. Additionally, The Home Depot will honor requests only from the original buyer.

According to HP’s website, the company will match the current pre-tax price for new comparable PCs and identical HP printer, display, and accessory model numbers from nationally recognized online retailers, such as Dell, Lenovo, Amazon, Staples, Newegg, and Best Buy. Item specs must be identical to get a price match, but the product model number does not need to be exact.

You must ask for the match at the time of purchase by calling 1-800-888-0229 or via chat. You can’t request a price match via email, and the policy doesn’t apply to items purchased during promotional periods. It also doesn’t apply to membership stores like Costco and Sam’s Club, items purchased through a third-party vendor on an approved site, or items purchased using financing.

HP also offers price protection on HP-branded products. If a price drops during your 30-day return window, you can request an adjustment by contacting HP’s Order Support team. This doesn’t apply to products purchased during certain seasonal sales events, including Fourth of July promotions and deals offered during the week of Thanksgiving and Cyber Monday.

Lenovo matches pricing on comparable PCs from Lenovo, HP, or Dell with the same key specs as long as they’re sold through a list of recognized retailers. That list includes bigger stores such as Best Buy, Walmart, Amazon, Newegg, and B&H Photo, as well as direct competitors such as Dell and HP. The comparable PC must also be in stock and readily available from both Lenovo and the competing retailer, and you must contact a Lenovo sales rep with specific details about the specs and availability of the product you’re inquiring about in order to facilitate the match.

That said, you can’t use coupons or special discounts when requesting a price match, and the policy doesn’t apply to membership stores like Costco. The policy also doesn’t apply to “Marketplace” items fulfilled by third parties, so double-check that you bought the product directly from the retailer. Build-your-own models are also ineligible for a price match. Lenovo’s separate Price Guarantee program, meanwhile, is limited to five eligible Lenovo products per customer per year.

Lenovo also offers a price guarantee refund for laptops and personal desktop computers bought from Lenovo.com within 30 days of purchase. To receive a refund, you must contact Customer Care and provide proof of the lower price for the identical product offered on Lenovo.com. This policy doesn’t apply to doorbuster or clearance products, however.

For the holidays, Lenovo has extended its price guarantee through January 27th, 2027, for eligible purchases made between November 2nd and December 13th. To request an adjustment, email a dated screenshot of the lower price to “na_lpg@lenovo.com” by January 27th.

Lowe’s will match the price of identical, in-stock products when you present an ad, product listing, or photo from a qualifying online or local retailer, including Amazon, Best Buy, Target, and Walmart. However, the product must be purchased directly from the retailer and not a third-party seller; it also doesn’t apply to special sales events like Black Friday and other holiday promotions.

Additionally, Lowe’s doesn’t match prices from one Lowe’s store to another, nor does it match any rebate discounts or those available only to a select group of customers (such as loyalty clubs). Clearance, seasonal, buy one get one free, and custom-made items are also ineligible for a price match. To request a price match, you can go to the store, call 1-877-465-6937, or chat online.

Normally, Microsoft will refund the difference if it drops the price of an in-stock physical product purchased online from the Microsoft Store within 60 days of delivery. For the holidays, however, Microsoft has extended that window through January 31st, 2027, for eligible purchases made between October 1st and December 2nd. The same 60-day policy also applies if you find the identical product on sale at a lower price at a qualifying retailer such as Amazon or Best Buy, though it doesn’t extend to the Surface Hub, HoloLens, Windows DevKits, or digital products.

Furthermore, some eligible products are subject to specific retailer exclusions. For example, price matches for Surface products at other retailers are limited to Amazon and Best Buy, while Xbox products are limited to those two retailers, as well as Target, GameStop, and Walmart. The price match policy excludes products from third-party sellers, clearance items, bundles, rebates, and prices only available after guests log in.

You’ll need to contact Microsoft’s Sales and Support team at 1-800-642-7676 within 60 days of delivery to request a refund, unless your purchase qualifies for the extended holiday price protection period. If you purchase an item from a Microsoft Experience Center, you’ll need to return to that location with your receipt. In both cases, Microsoft will credit the difference between the price you paid and the current lower price to your original payment method, which typically takes between three and five days to process.

Newegg no longer matches prices from competing retailers. The company only price matches eligible items sold and shipped by Newegg (not third-party sellers), and the product must be identical and in stock. The lower price must also appear on Newegg.com within seven calendar days of your purchase. If approved, Newegg will issue the difference as credit you can use on the site or in the app for up to 365 days.

The match doesn’t apply to rebates, coupons, financing offers, bundles, clearance items, open-box items, or refurbished products. The policy also doesn’t apply to prices found at third-party marketplace sellers, or products on sale during major sales events like Black Friday and Cyber Monday.

You can currently request a price match against other Sam’s Club stores, but not competitors. Note that this doesn’t apply to items marked down or on clearance.
